Reused by ``eval doctor``\n    so the doctor output can enumerate matchers without re-reading the\n    file from disk.\n\nWiring\n======\n\n``superclaude eval doctor --check-coverage`` invokes the gate against\nthe doctor-scoped suite and ``~/.claude/settings.json``; a failure\nprints the missing-pattern roster on stderr and exits 2 (alongside\nHARD-capability failures).\n\n``superclaude eval run`` invokes the gate at the top of the run AFTER\nsuite parse and BEFORE any worker is dispatched, so a missing-coverage\nbreach short-circuits the run without touching any per-eval HOME.\nArtifacts land inside the resolved ``--output-dir`` so the FR-G4 run\ndirectory contains the full forensic trail.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport re\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Callable, Iterable, Mapping, Optional, Sequence\n\nfrom .models import EvalSpec\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"COVERAGE_GATE_FAILED_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"COVERAGE_MISSING_ARTIFACT_PREFIX\",\n    \"CoverageMatcher\",\n    \"CoverageResult\",\n    \"coverage_gate\",\n    \"default_matcher_filter\",\n    \"eval_covers_pattern\",\n    \"extract_hook_matchers\",\n    \"sanitize_pattern_for_filename\",\n]\n\n\nCOVERAGE_GATE_FAILED_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when the gate reports missing coverage.\n\nDesign-spec §4 maps every harness-rejection outcome (schema error,\nscratch-root violation, missing capability, missing matcher coverage,\netc.) to exit code ``2``. Pinned here so the CLI wiring and the\nT04.14 / T05.25 tests agree on a single source of truth.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nCOVERAGE_MISSING_ARTIFACT_PREFIX: str = \"coverage_missing:\"\n\"\"\"Filename prefix for the per-pattern artifact written on a breach.\n\nThe full filename is ``coverage_missing:<sanitised-pattern>`` so a\n``grep -l '^coverage_missing:'`` against the run directory surfaces\nevery uncovered matcher in deterministic order. The colon is\nfilesystem-safe on every POSIX host the harness supports; the\nsanitiser swaps out characters that are not (``/``, ``|``, ``*``, …)\nso the artifact can also be tar'd up without escaping.\n\"\"\"\n\n\n# v1 covers these three MCP tool prefixes per design-spec §1.5. The\n# tuple is module-private because the default filter is exported as a\n# function — callers that need to extend coverage compose their own\n# predicate rather than mutating this list.\n_DEFAULT_MCP_TOOL_PREFIXES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"mcp__auggie__\",\n    \"mcp__auggie-mcp__\",\n    \"mcp__airis-mcp-gateway__\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass CoverageMatcher:\n    \"\"\"One matcher pattern extracted from ``settings.json``.\n\n    Attributes:\n        event: Hook event the matcher is bound to (``PreToolUse``,\n            ``PostToolUse``, ``UserPromptSubmit``, …). Carried so the\n            artifact and stderr output can name the registration site.\n        pattern: The matcher regex as written in settings.json\n            (verbatim, not normalised). Coverage resolution compiles\n            this string with :func:`re.compile`; an unparseable pattern\n            yields ``covers=False`` for every eval.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    event: str\n    pattern: str\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass CoverageResult:\n    \"\"\"Outcome of a single :func:`coverage_gate` invocation.\n\n    Attributes:\n        matchers: Every matcher considered (post ``matcher_filter``).\n        covered: Subset of ``matchers`` that has at least one covering\n            eval. Order matches ``matchers``.\n        missing: Subset of ``matchers`` with no covering eval. The gate\n            fails (``passed=False``) iff this is non-empty.\n        artifacts: Mapping of pattern → on-disk artifact path written\n            during this invocation. Empty when ``output_dir`` is\n            ``None`` or when no matcher is missing coverage.\n        coverage_map: Mapping of pattern → tuple of covering eval ids.\n            Always populated (even when the gate passes) so callers can\n            render the full coverage roster.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    matchers: tuple[CoverageMatcher, ...] = ()\n    covered: tuple[CoverageMatcher, ...] = ()\n    missing: tuple[CoverageMatcher, ...] = ()\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, Path] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    coverage_map: Mapping[str, tuple[str, ...]] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n    @property\n    def passed(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` iff every checked matcher has a covering eval.\"\"\"\n        return not self.missing\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]:\n        \"\"\"JSON-serialisable view for ``eval doctor --json`` payloads.\"\"\"\n        return {\n            \"passed\": self.passed,\n            \"matchers\": [\n                {\"event\": m.event, \"pattern\": m.pattern} for m in self.matchers\n            ],\n            \"covered\": [\n                {\"event\": m.event, \"pattern\": m.pattern} for m in self.covered\n            ],\n            \"missing\": [\n                {\"event\": m.event, \"pattern\": m.pattern} for m in self.missing\n            ],\n            \"artifacts\": {k: str(v) for k, v in self.artifacts.items()},\n            \"coverage_map\": {\n                k: list(v) for k, v in self.coverage_map.items()\n            },\n        }\n\n\ndef sanitize_pattern_for_filename(pattern: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Replace filesystem-unsafe characters in a matcher pattern.\n\n    Permits ``[A-Za-z0-9_:.-]`` verbatim; everything else (``/``, ``|``,\n    ``*``, whitespace) collapses to ``_``. The colon is kept because it\n    is filesystem-safe on every POSIX host the harness supports and the\n    artifact prefix already uses one.\n    \"\"\"\n    return re.sub(r\"[^A-Za-z0-9_:.-]\", \"_\", pattern)\n\n\ndef default_matcher_filter(pattern: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"v1 only enforces coverage for matchers that target MCP tools.\n\n    Matchers like ``Edit|Write|mcp__serena__replace_content`` mix MCP\n    tool prefixes with built-in tool names — these are bookkeeping\n    hooks that no eval needs to issue a tool call for. The v1 gate\n    therefore restricts itself to matchers that mention one of the\n    three known auggie/airis MCP prefixes; T05.25 extends the\n    predicate as additional MCP tool families come online.\n    \"\"\"\n    return any(prefix in pattern for prefix in _DEFAULT_MCP_TOOL_PREFIXES)\n\n\ndef extract_hook_matchers(\n    settings: Mapping[str, object],\n) -> tuple[CoverageMatcher, ...]:\n    \"\"\"Walk ``hooks.<event>[].matcher`` entries from parsed settings.json.\n\n    Entries without a ``matcher`` field (e.g. ``SessionStart`` hooks\n    that always fire) are skipped — the gate has nothing to verify for\n    them. The returned tuple preserves declaration order so the\n    artifact list and stderr roster are deterministic across runs.\n    \"\"\"\n    hooks = settings.get(\"hooks\")\n    if not isinstance(hooks, Mapping):\n        return ()\n    out: list[CoverageMatcher] = []\n    for event, entries in hooks.items():\n        if isinstance(entries, (str, bytes)) or not isinstance(entries, Iterable):\n            continue\n        for entry in entries:\n            if not isinstance(entry, Mapping):\n                continue\n            pattern = entry.get(\"matcher\")\n            if isinstance(pattern, str) and pattern:\n                out.append(CoverageMatcher(event=str(event), pattern=pattern))\n    return tuple(out)\n\n\ndef _iter_eval_tool_calls(spec: EvalSpec) -> Iterable[str]:\n    \"\"\"Yield tool-call names this eval issues.\n\n    The v1 mapping rule reads ``inputs[].expect_tool_call`` (a string\n    declared on each input row that names the MCP tool the eval is\n    expected to invoke). Future tag conventions (``covers:`` field,\n    ``provides:`` list) can be added without changing the gate\n    surface; for now this single derivation rule keeps the registry\n    aligned with what evals already declare.\n    \"\"\"\n    for row in spec.inputs:\n        if not isinstance(row, Mapping):\n            continue\n        tc = row.get(\"expect_tool_call\")\n        if isinstance(tc, str) and tc:\n            yield tc\n\n\ndef eval_covers_pattern(spec: EvalSpec, pattern: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return ``True`` if ``spec`` issues any tool call matched by ``pattern``.\n\n    ``pattern`` is compiled with :func:`re.search` so a matcher like\n    ``mcp__auggie__.*`` correctly anchors against the prefix. An\n    unparseable pattern returns ``False`` rather than raising so the\n    gate can still report it as ``missing`` (with the regex error\n    surfaced via the artifact payload).\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        regex = re.compile(pattern)\n    except re.error:\n        return False\n    return any(regex.search(tc) for tc in _iter_eval_tool_calls(spec))\n\n\ndef coverage_gate(\n    settings_path: Path,\n    suite: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n    *,\n    output_dir: Optional[Path] = None,\n    matcher_filter: Optional[Callable[[str], bool]] = None,\n) -> CoverageResult:\n    \"\"\"Compute matcher → eval coverage and emit per-pattern artifacts.\n\n    Args:\n        settings_path: Path to a ``settings.json`` containing the hook\n            registrations to enforce coverage over. The\n        ``coverage_map`` reports the resolved eval ids per pattern so\n        callers can render the full registry alongside the verdict.\n    \"\"\"\n    matcher_filter = matcher_filter or default_matcher_filter\n    if not settings_path.is_file():\n        return CoverageResult()\n    try:\n        data = json.loads(settings_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n        return CoverageResult()\n    if not isinstance(data, Mapping):\n        return CoverageResult()\n\n    raw = extract_hook_matchers(data)\n    matchers = tuple(m for m covered.append(m)\n        else:\n            missing.append(m)\n\n    artifacts: dict[str, Path] = {}\n    if missing and output_dir is not None:\n        output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        for m in missing:\n            filename = (\n                f\"{COVERAGE_MISSING_ARTIFACT_PREFIX}\"\n                f\"{sanitize_pattern_for_filename(m.pattern)}\"\n            )\n            path = output_dir / filename\n            payload = {\n                \"event\": m.event,\n                \"pattern\": m.pattern,\n                \"coverage_missing\": True,\n ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Output dir + AC12 scratch-root allowlist\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    base_config = EvalConfig()\n    run_id = _new_run_id()\n    requested_output = (\n        output_dir if output_dir is not None else _default_output_dir(run_id)\n    )\n\n    try:\n        resolved_output = resolve_scratch_root(\n            requested_output,\n            config=base_config,\n            output_dir=output_dir,\n        )\n    except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n        click.echo(format_scratch_root_violation(exc), err=True)\n        sys.exit(SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    resolved_output.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    home_root = resolved_output / \"homes\"\n    home_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    # The runtime EvalConfig extends the canonical allowlist with the\n    # per-run home root + the operator-supplied output directory so\n    # downstream ``containment_guard`` calls see a stable allowlist\n    # rather than re-deriving one from scratch.\n    runtime_allowed = tuple(base_config.allowed_scratch_roots) + (\n        resolved_output,\n        home_root,\n    )\n    runtime_config = EvalConfig(\n        paths=base_config.paths,\n        defaults=base_config.defaults,\n        allowed_scratch_roots=runtime_allowed,\n        min_claude_version=base_config.min_claude_version,\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Suite resolution + parse + post-expansion filter\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    try:\n        manifest_path = resolve_suite_manifest(suite, _DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR)\n    except SuiteNotFound as exc:\n        click.echo(f\"eval run: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    try:\n        parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n    except SuiteLoaderError as exc:\n        click.echo(f\"eval run: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    manifest_n = len(parsed.evals)\n    specs: tuple[EvalSpec, ...] = parsed.evals\n    if eval_ids:\n        wanted = set(eval_ids)\n        filtered = tuple(s for s in specs if s.id in wanted)\n        missing = wanted - {s.id for s in filtered}\n        if missing:","numLines":220,"startLine":1255,"totalLines":1647}}}

Callers that mint scratch directories,\n177\t    per-eval HOMEs, or output dirs MUST route the target through this\n178\t    function *before* any filesystem write so a malformed path cannot\n179\t    leak side effects onto disk.\n180\t\n181\t    Args:\n182\t        path: Candidate scratch path. ``str`` accepted for ergonomics;\n183\t            normalised to ``Path``. The value is resolved the same\n194\t            way as the rest of the allowlist; passing it does NOT mutate\n195\t            ``config``.\n196\t\n197\t    Returns:\n198\t        The resolved absolute :class:`~pathlib.Path` for ``path``. Callers\n199\t        SHOULD use this returned value rather than the original input when\n200\t        composing further sub-paths so subsequent comparisons stay stable.\n201\t\n202\t    Raises:\n203\t        ScratchRootViolation: ``path`` (after resolution) is not equal to\n204\t            and not relative to any entry in the resolved allowlist. The\n205\t            exception carries the original input, the resolved form, and\n206\t            the resolved allowlist so reporters can render verbatim\n207\t            forensics. Callers that mint scratch directories,\n    per-eval HOMEs, or output dirs MUST route the target through this\n    function *before* any filesystem write so a malformed path cannot\n    leak side effects onto disk.\n\n    Args:\n        path: Candidate scratch path. ``str`` accepted for ergonomics;\n            normalised to ``Path``. The value is expanded (``~``) and\n            resolved (``strict=False``) so symlinks and relative segments\n            do not escape the allowlist.\n        config: Optional :class:`EvalConfig` whose\n            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` provides the policy allowlist. Falls\n            back to a default-constructed instance, which yields the\n            canonical pair ``(/tmp/eval-runs, .dev/eval-runs)``.\n            Passing the project's live ``EvalConfig`` guarantees there is\n            exactly one source of truth — no other module embeds a copy.\n        output_dir: Optional CLI-supplied ``--output-dir`` to extend the\n            allowlist for this call only. The value is resolved the same\n            way as the rest of the allowlist; passing it does NOT mutate\n            ``config``.\n\n    Returns:\n        The resolved absolute :class:`~pathlib.Path` for ``path``. Callers\n        SHOULD use this returned value rather than the original input when\n        composing further sub-paths so subsequent comparisons stay stable.\n\n    Raises:\n        ScratchRootViolation: ``path`` (after resolution) is not equal to\n            and not relative to any entry in the resolved allowlist. The\n            exception carries the original input, the resolved form, and\n            the resolved allowlist so reporters can render verbatim\n            forensics. CLI callers MUST map it to\n            :data:`SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    config = config if config is not None else EvalConfig()\n\n    # Build the allowlist from EvalConfig (sole source of truth) plus the\n    # optional per-call --output-dir. Resolving each prefix once here keeps\n    # the comparison loop below allocation-free.\n    allowed: list[Path] = [\n        _resolve_prefix(prefix) for prefix in config.allowed_scratch_roots\n    ]\n    if output_dir is not None:\n        allowed.append(_resolve_prefix(Path(output_dir)))\n\n    candidate = Path(path)\n    resolved = candidate.expanduser().resolve(strict=False)\n\n    for prefix in allowed:\n        # ``is_relative_to`` catches strict sub-paths; the equality branch\n        # accepts the prefix itself (``/tmp/eval-runs`` is a valid root).\n        if resolved == prefix or resolved.is_relative_to(prefix):\n            return resolved\n\n    raise ScratchRootViolation(candidate, resolved, allowed)\n\n\ndef format_scratch_root_violation(exc: ScratchRootViolation) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a :class:`ScratchRootViolation` with the canonical policy text.\n","numLines":70,"startLine":167,"totalLines":261}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QzHerdx9PHNk4B7duXQfuP","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0101 — R3-mit MCP Retry-Once Policy\n2\t\n3\t| Field | Value |\n4\t|---|---|\n5\t| Task | T05.23 |\n6\t| Component(s) | `src/superclaude/cli/eval/retry.py` (new), `src/superclaude/cli/eval/runner.py` (EvalRunner), `docs/eval/retry.md`, `tests/cli/eval/test_mcp_retry_once.py` |\n7\t| Roadmap | R-100 (R3-mit) |\n8\t| Spec refs | design-spec §13 (Bounded retry), §14 (R3 mitigation), decisions.md §B (OQ-10 closure) |\n9\t| Depends on | D-0049 (EvalRunner class, T03.05), D-0051 (NFR-REL2 bounded retry, T03.08) |\n10\t| Consumed by | Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) — surfaces `mcp_server_flaky` artifact in summary tables |\n11\t\n12\t## 1. Goal\n13\t\n14\tLand the **opt-in retry-once branch** the design-spec §14 reserves\n15\tfor MCP-flaky evals. Concretely:\n16\t\n17\t> *On the first non-PASS outcome whose status matches the flaky\n18\t> taxonomy (FAIL, ERRORED, TIMEOUT) AND whose spec carries the\n19\t> `MCP-flaky` capability tag, the runner re-executes the per-eval\n20\t> lifecycle exactly once. The final outcome — whether the retry\n21\t> recovered or the failure persisted — carries the\n22\t> `mcp_server_flaky` artifact in `outcome.artifacts`.*\n23\t\n24\tFour deliverables in scope:\n25\t\n26\t1. New module `src/superclaude/cli/eval/retry.py` exporting\n27\t   `RetryOncePolicy`, `MCP_FLAKY_TAG`, `MCP_SERVER_FLAKY_ARTIFACT`,\n28\t   `is_mcp_flaky_tagged`, `is_flaky_outcome`.\n29\t2. `EvalRunner` integration: additive `retry_policy` and\n30\t   `home_factory` kwargs; `run()` consults the policy after each\n31\t   first attempt and re-invokes the lifecycle via `_execute_once()`\n32\t   when the policy elects retry.\n33\t3. `docs/eval/retry.md` update reflecting the R3-mit contract: the\n34\t   taxonomy table, manifest example, orthogonality with `retry_count`,\n35\t   and OQ-10 closure note.\n36\t4. `tests/cli/eval/test_mcp_retry_once.py` regression suite (26\n37\t   tests) covering policy unit behaviour, EvalRunner integration, and\n38\t   compatibility with the NFR-REL2 `retry_count` rejection.\n39\t\n40\tThe policy is **stateless and immutable** (frozen dataclass) — the\n41\trunner tracks attempt count externally so a stateful policy bug\n42\tcannot loop.\n43\t\n44\t## 2. Public Surface\n45\t\n46\t```python\n47\tfrom superclaude.cli.eval.retry import (\n48\t    MCP_FLAKY_TAG,                # \"MCP-flaky\" (mirrors EvalRunner.MCP_FLAKY_TAG)\n49\t    MCP_SERVER_FLAKY_ARTIFACT,    # \"mcp_server_flaky\"\n50\t    RetryOncePolicy,              # frozen dataclass; MAX_ATTEMPTS == 2\n51\t    is_mcp_flaky_tagged,          # (EvalSpec) -> bool\n52\t    is_flaky_outcome,             # (EvalOutcome) -> bool\n53\t)\n54\tfrom superclaude.cli.eval.runner import EvalRunner\n55\t\n56\tEvalRunner(\n57\t    ...,\n58\t    retry_policy=RetryOncePolicy(),    # opt-in; default None disables R3-mit\n59\t    home_factory=lambda: HomeIsolation(...),  # required in prod (setup is not re-entrant)\n60\t)\n61\t```\n62\t\n63\t### 2.1 RetryOncePolicy contract\n64\t\n65\t| Method | Returns | Contract |\n66\t|---|---|---|\n67\t| `is_eligible(spec)` | `bool` | `True` iff `MCP_FLAKY_TAG in spec.requires`. |\n68\t| `should_retry(spec, outcome)` | `bool` | `True` iff `is_eligible(spec)` AND `is_flaky_outcome(outcome)`. The runner consults this only on the *first* attempt's outcome. |\n69\t| `annotate(outcome)` | `EvalOutcome` | Returns a new outcome with `mcp_server_flaky=\"true\"` added to `outcome.artifacts`. Idempotent: re-application returns the input unchanged when the artifact is already present. |\n70\t\n71\t### 2.2 Flaky taxonomy\n72\t\n73\t| Status | Eligible | Rationale |\n74\t|---|---|---|\n75\t| `FAIL` | Yes | MCP timeout expressed through expect callable |\n76\t| `ERRORED` | Yes | Harness exception (often MCP transport) |\n77\t| `TIMEOUT` | Yes | Most common MCP-flake signature |\n78\t| `PASS` | No | Nothing to retry |\n79\t| `SKIPPED` | No | Capability gate — did not run |\n80\t| `INTERRUPTED` | No | SIGINT — operator intent |\n81\t| `XFAIL` / `XPASS` | No | Expected-failure contract |\n82\t\n83\t### 2.3 EvalRunner integration\n84\t\n85\t`EvalRunner.run(spec)` now wraps `_execute_once(spec)` in a single\n86\tpolicy-gated retry branch:\n87\t\n88\t```python\n89\toutcome = self._execute_once(spec)\n90\tif self._retry_policy is not None and self._retry_policy.should_retry(spec, outcome):\n91\t    if self._home_factory is not None:\n92\t        self._home = self._home_factory()\n93\t    retry_outcome = self._execute_once(spec)\n94\t    outcome = self._retry_policy.annotate(retry_outcome)\n95\treturn outcome\n96\t```\n97\t\n98\t`home_factory` is consulted only when wired. Production callers MUST\n99\twire one because `HomeIsolation.setup()` raises `RuntimeError` on a\n100\tsecond call without an intervening teardown — the factory produces a\n101\tfresh `HomeIsolation` so the second lifecycle sees the same contract.\n102\tTest stubs whose `setup()` is callable across teardown may omit the\n103\tfactory.\n104\t\n105\t## 3. Behavioural Contract\n106\t\n107\t### 3.1 Retry-once invariant\n108\t\n109\tFor every `EvalSpec` passed to `EvalRunner.run`:\n110\t\n111\t* If `retry_policy is None`: the executor trio fires **exactly once**\n112\t  (NFR-REL2 default).\n113\t* If `retry_policy` is wired AND `should_retry()` returns `False`:\n114\t  the executor trio fires **exactly once**.\n115\t* If `retry_policy` is wired AND `should_retry()` returns `True`:\n116\t  the executor trio fires **exactly twice** — the original attempt\n117\t  plus one retry. The policy never re-consults `should_retry()` on\n118\t  the retry attempt's outcome.\n119\t\n120\t### 3.2 Artifact attachment\n121\t\n122\tThe `mcp_server_flaky` artifact is attached **after** the retry\n123\tattempt completes, regardless of whether the retry's status is `PASS`,\n124\t`FAIL`, `ERRORED`, or `TIMEOUT`. The artifact records *the fact\n125\tthat a retry occurred*, not the result of the retry.\n126\t\n127\t| Scenario | First attempt | Retry attempt | Final status | Artifact present? |\n128\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n129\t| Non-tagged eval, fails | FAIL | — | FAIL | No |\n130\t| Tagged eval, passes | PASS | — | PASS | No |\n131\t| Tagged eval, fails then passes | FAIL | PASS | PASS | Yes |\n132\t| Tagged eval, fails persistently | FAIL | FAIL | FAIL | Yes |\n133\t| Tagged eval, errors then errors | ERRORED | ERRORED | ERRORED | Yes |\n134\t| Tagged eval, times out then succeeds | TIMEOUT | PASS | PASS | Yes |\n135\t\n136\t### 3.3 Orthogonality with NFR-REL2\n137\t\n138\t| Construction | Behaviour |\n139\t|---|---|\n140\t| `retry_count=0`, `retry_policy=None` | NFR-REL2 default — no retry under any condition. |\n141\t| `retry_count=0`, `retry_policy=RetryOncePolicy()` | R3-mit policy active for tagged evals only. |\n142\t| `retry_count=1`, `retry_policy=None` | `ValueError` at construction time (NFR-REL2 guard). |\n143\t| `retry_count=1`, `retry_policy=RetryOncePolicy()` | `ValueError` at construction time (NFR-REL2 guard not relaxed by policy presence). |\n144\t\n145\t## 4. Goal\n\nLand the **opt-in retry-once branch** the design-spec §14 reserves\nfor MCP-flaky evals. Concretely:\n\n> *On the first non-PASS outcome whose status matches the flaky\n> taxonomy (FAIL, ERRORED, TIMEOUT) AND whose spec carries the\n> `MCP-flaky` capability tag, the runner re-executes the per-eval\n> lifecycle exactly once. The final outcome — whether the retry\n> recovered or the failure persisted — carries the\n> `mcp_server_flaky` artifact in `outcome.artifacts`.*\n\nFour deliverables in scope:\n\n1. New module `src/superclaude/cli/eval/retry.py` exporting\n   `RetryOncePolicy`, `MCP_FLAKY_TAG`, `MCP_SERVER_FLAKY_ARTIFACT`,\n   `is_mcp_flaky_tagged`, `is_flaky_outcome`.\n2. `EvalRunner` integration: additive `retry_policy` and\n   `home_factory` kwargs; `run()` consults the policy after each\n   first attempt and re-invokes the lifecycle via `_execute_once()`\n   when the policy elects retry.\n3. `docs/eval/retry.md` update reflecting the R3-mit contract: the\n   taxonomy table, manifest example, orthogonality with `retry_count`,\n   and OQ-10 closure note.\n4. `tests/cli/eval/test_mcp_retry_once.py` regression suite (26\n   tests) covering policy unit behaviour, EvalRunner integration, and\n   compatibility with the NFR-REL2 `retry_count` rejection.\n\nThe policy is **stateless and immutable** (frozen dataclass) — the\nrunner tracks attempt count externally so a stateful policy bug\ncannot loop.\n\n## 2. Public Surface\n\n```python\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.retry import (\n    MCP_FLAKY_TAG,                # \"MCP-flaky\" (mirrors EvalRunner.MCP_FLAKY_TAG)\n    MCP_SERVER_FLAKY_ARTIFACT,    # \"mcp_server_flaky\"\n    RetryOncePolicy,              # frozen dataclass; MAX_ATTEMPTS == 2\n    is_mcp_flaky_tagged,          # (EvalSpec) -> bool\n    is_flaky_outcome,             # (EvalOutcome) -> bool\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.runner import EvalRunner\n\nEvalRunner(\n    ...,\n    retry_policy=RetryOncePolicy(),    # opt-in; default None disables R3-mit\n    home_factory=lambda: HomeIsolation(...),  # required in prod (setup is not re-entrant)\n)\n```\n\n### 2.1 RetryOncePolicy contract\n\n| Method | Returns | Contract |\n|---|---|---|\n| `is_eligible(spec)` | `bool` | `True` iff `MCP_FLAKY_TAG in spec.requires`. |\n| `should_retry(spec, outcome)` | `bool` | `True` iff `is_eligible(spec)` AND `is_flaky_outcome(outcome)`. The runner consults this only on the *first* attempt's outcome. |\n| `annotate(outcome)` | `EvalOutcome` | Returns a new outcome with `mcp_server_flaky=\"true\"` added to `outcome.artifacts`. Idempotent: re-application returns the input unchanged when the artifact is already present. |\n\n### 2.2 Flaky taxonomy\n\n| Status | Eligible | Rationale |\n|---|---|---|\n| `FAIL` | Yes | MCP timeout expressed through expect callable |\n| `ERRORED` | Yes | Harness exception (often MCP transport) |\n| `TIMEOUT` | Yes | Most common MCP-flake signature |\n| `PASS` | No | Nothing to retry |\n| `SKIPPED` | No | Capability gate — did not run |\n| `INTERRUPTED` | No | SIGINT — operator intent |\n| `XFAIL` / `XPASS` | No | Expected-failure contract |\n\n### 2.3 EvalRunner integration\n\n`EvalRunner.run(spec)` now wraps `_execute_once(spec)` in a single\npolicy-gated retry branch:\n\n```python\noutcome = self._execute_once(spec)\nif self._retry_policy is not None and self._retry_policy.should_retry(spec, outcome):\n    if self._home_factory is not None:\n        self._home = self._home_factory()\n    retry_outcome = self._execute_once(spec)\n    outcome = self._retry_policy.annotate(retry_outcome)\nreturn outcome\n```\n\n`home_factory` is consulted only when wired. Production callers MUST\nwire one because `HomeIsolation.setup()` raises `RuntimeError` on a\nsecond call without an intervening teardown — the factory produces a\nfresh `HomeIsolation` so the second lifecycle sees the same contract.\nTest stubs whose `setup()` is callable across teardown may omit the\nfactory.\n\n## 3. Behavioural Contract\n\n### 3.1 Retry-once invariant\n\nFor every `EvalSpec` passed to `EvalRunner.run`:\n\n* If `retry_policy is None`: the executor trio fires **exactly once**\n  (NFR-REL2 default).\n* If `retry_policy` is wired AND `should_retry()` returns `False`:\n  the executor trio fires **exactly once**.\n* If `retry_policy` is wired AND `should_retry()` returns `True`:\n  the executor trio fires **exactly twice** — the original attempt\n  plus one retry. The policy never re-consults `should_retry()` on\n  the retry attempt's outcome.\n\n### 3.2 Artifact attachment\n\nThe `mcp_server_flaky` artifact is attached **after** the retry\nattempt completes, regardless of whether the retry's status is `PASS`,\n`FAIL`, `ERRORED`, or `TIMEOUT`. The artifact records *the fact\nthat a retry occurred*, not the result of the retry.\n\n| Scenario | First attempt | Retry attempt | Final status | Artifact present? |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Non-tagged eval, fails | FAIL | — | FAIL | No |\n| Tagged eval, passes | PASS | — | PASS | No |\n| Tagged eval, fails then passes | FAIL | PASS | PASS | Yes |\n| Tagged eval, fails persistently | FAIL | FAIL | FAIL | Yes |\n| Tagged eval, errors then errors | ERRORED | ERRORED | ERRORED | Yes |\n| Tagged eval, times out then succeeds | TIMEOUT | PASS | PASS | Yes |\n\n### 3.3 Orthogonality with NFR-REL2\n\n| Construction | Behaviour |\n|---|---|\n| `retry_count=0`, `retry_policy=None` | NFR-REL2 default — no retry under any condition. |\n| `retry_count=0`, `retry_policy=RetryOncePolicy()` | R3-mit policy active for tagged evals only. |\n| `retry_count=1`, `retry_policy=None` | `ValueError` at construction time (NFR-REL2 guard). |\n| `retry_count=1`, `retry_policy=RetryOncePolicy()` | `ValueError` at construction time (NFR-REL2 guard not relaxed by policy presence). |\n\n## 4. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CKT56Yd7fXneHbBsCkXBg8","type":"tool_result","content":"1008\tdef resolve_suite_manifest(suite: str, suites_dir: Path) -> Path:\n1009\t    \"\"\"Resolve ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n1010\t\n1011\t    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n1012\t\n1013\t    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n1014\t    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n1015\t       convention used by the built-in suites).\n1016\t    3. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012bAVS4syopbcNSWJD4yi73","type":"tool_result","content":"152\t        )\n153\t\n154\t\n155\tdef _resolve_prefix(prefix: Path) -> Path:\n156\t    \"\"\"Resolve an allowlist entry to its absolute, symlink-free form.\n157\t\n158\t    ``EvalConfig`` ships relative defaults (``.dev/eval-runs``) so the\n159\t    repo-local prefix anchors against the process CWD. ``strict=False``\n160\t    keeps the helper usable in tests / fresh checkouts where the scratch\n161\t    directory may not yet exist.\n162\t    \"\"\"\n163\t\n164\t    return Path(prefix).expanduser().resolve(strict=False)\n165\t\n166\t\n167\tdef resolve_scratch_root(\n168\t    path: Path | str,\n169\t    *,\n170\t    config: EvalConfig | None = None,\n171\t    output_dir: Path | str | None = None,\n172\t) -> Path:\n173\t    \"\"\"Resolve ``path`` and verify it lives under an AC12 allowed root.\n174\t\n175\t    The helper is the single ingress point for the AC12 allowlist policy\n176\t    (see roadmap R-016 / FR-ISO2).
